=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    //return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return 'counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i - truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res

        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    //return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return 'counter

        return res
```

- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.

- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.

```
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        forbidden = set(forbidden)
        l = 0
        ans = 0
        for r in range(len(word)):
            for i in range(r, max(r-10, l-1), -1):
                if word[i:r+1] in forbidden:
                    l = i+1
                    break
            ans = max(ans, r-l+1)
        return ans
```

```
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            node = trie
            for ch in f:
                if ch not in node:
                    node[ch] = {}
                node = node[ch]
            node['#'] = True

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        left = 0
        for right in range(n):
            node = trie
            for i in range(right, left-1, -1):
                ch = word[i]
                if ch not in node:
                    break
                node = node[ch]
                if '#' in node:
                    left = i + 1
                    break
            ans = max(ans, right-left+1)
        return ans
```

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}

def isForbidden(l, r):
    t = trie
    for pos in range(l, r):
        c = word[pos]
        if c not in t:
            return False
        t = t[c]
        if "end" in t:
            return pos - l + 1
    return False
```

Define for each position i, `j_i` after processing positions from n-1 down to i. Claim `j_i = min( n, min_{s >= i, forbidden word occurs at s} (s + len - 1) )`. That is the earliest exclusive end among forbidden substrings starting at or after i. Then the longest valid substring starting at i has length `j_i - i`, because any substring word[i:j] is valid iff j <= j_i. Need prove by induction.

Base i=n: j_n = n, no starts >=n, min empty = n.

Inductive step from i+1 to i. We have j_{i+1} = min over s>=i+1 of end_exclusive. For i, the only new forbidden substrings are those starting at i. Let L be the minimum length among forbidden words starting at i? Actually if multiple forbidden words start at i, the one with smallest L gives the smallest end_exclusive = i+L-1. `isForbidden(word[i:j_{i+1}])` traverses trie and returns the first forbidden prefix encountered, which is the shortest forbidden word starting at i that is fully contained in word[i:j_{i+1}]. If that L satisfies i+L <= j_{i+1}, then it returns L. If no forbidden word starting at i is fully contained in the current window, then no new restriction because any forbidden word starting at i with length L has i+L > j_{i+1}, so i+L-1 >= j_{i+1}, so its end_exclusive is >= j_{i+1}; it doesn't lower the min. Thus if `truc` false, j_i = j_{i+1}. If true, new candidate = i+truc-1. Since i+truc <= j_{i+1}, candidate < j_{i+1}, so j_i = candidate. So truc is the minimum length among forbidden words starting at i that are prefixes of word[i:j]. If a shorter forbidden word starts at i but is not a prefix of word? Impossible, starting at i means it must match word. So truc is min length. Good.

Now, need decide final code style. We can output:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(l: int, r: int):
            t = trie
            for pos in range(l, r):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - l + 1
            return False

        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(i, j)
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

Need ensure `isForbidden` return type can be bool or int. In Python, `False` is comparable to int? `if truc:` works. `j = i + truc - 1` if truc False? We only if truc. Good.

Could include `max_len` optimization:
```
max_len = max(len(f) for f in forbidden)
...
for pos in range(l, min(r, l + max_len)):
```
But if `max_len` is 0? forbidden nonempty. If max_len maybe 10. This prevents traversing beyond longest forbidden. But is it necessary? If no forbidden prefix within max_len, return False. However, if `r-l` is huge and trie path matches a long prefix but no end, and max_len is smaller than that path? Actually max_len is max forbidden length, so any forbidden word has length <= max_len. If we only traverse up to max_len, we will see any forbidden prefix that starts at i and is contained in word[i:j] because its length <= max_len and <= j-i. Now, let's decide final code exactly.

Option 1: Minimal fix with slicing:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return counter
            return False

        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```
This is closer to original. But slicing O(n^2). If the judge has large constraints, TLE. The prompt didn't mention constraints, but LeetCode problem does. Should we output optimized index-based? The user asked "faulty python code" not "optimize". But "fix the code" in LeetCode context means pass tests. I'd prefer index-based.

Option 2: Index-based with max_len:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(l: int, r: int):
            t = trie
            limit = min(r, l + max_len)
            for pos in range(l, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - l + 1
            return False

        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(i, j)
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

Let's write final code cleanly:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            node = trie
            for ch in f:
                if ch not in node:
                    node[ch] = {}
                node = node[ch]
            node["end"] = True

        def forbidden_prefix_length(left: int, right: int) -> int:
            node = trie
            limit = min(right, left + max_len)
            for pos in range(left, limit):
                ch = word[pos]
                if ch not in node:
                    return 0
                node = node[ch]
                if "end" in node:
                    return pos - left + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        right = len(word)
        for left in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_length(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)
        return ans
```

This uses `return 0` instead of False. `if length:` works. `right` variable is exclusive boundary. This is clear.

Need ensure if `forbidden_prefix_length` returns 0 for no forbidden, and if `length` is 0, no update. Good.

Let `bad[i]` = minimum exclusive end `e` such that `word[i:e]` contains a forbidden substring? Actually for fixed start i, the longest valid prefix is until just before the earliest ending forbidden substring that starts at or after i. Let `limit[i] = min({n} ∪ {s + len(f) - 1 | f forbidden, s >= i, word[s:s+len(f)] == f})`. Then any substring `word[i:j]` is valid iff `j <= limit[i]`. Why? If j > limit[i], then there exists forbidden f at s >= i with s+len(f)-1 < j? Wait limit is min end_excl. If j > limit, then j >= limit+1. Let f at s with end_excl = limit. Then s+len(f)-1 = limit-1? Wait end_excl = s+len(f)-1. If j > end_excl, then j >= end_excl+1 = s+len(f), so substring includes f. Invalid. If j <= limit, then for every forbidden f at s>=i, end_excl = s+len(f)-1 >= limit >= j, so j <= s+len(f)-1, meaning substring ends before or at last char of f? Let's check: if j = s+len(f)-1, substring includes indices up to s+len(f)-2, missing last char, so valid. If j = s+len(f), invalid. So yes. Thus max valid length = limit[i] - i.

Algorithm computes limit[i] from right to left. Initially limit[n] = n. For i, limit[i] = min(limit[i+1], min_{f starting at i} (i+len(f)-1)). The inner min over f starting at i is just the length of the shortest forbidden word starting at i, if any, because end_excl = i+L-1, minimized by smallest L. `forbidden_prefix_length(i, limit[i+1])` finds the shortest forbidden word starting at i that is fully contained in word[i:limit[i+1]], i.e., with i+L <= limit[i+1]. If no such word, then any forbidden word starting at i has i+L > limit[i+1], so i+L-1 >= limit[i+1], so it doesn't reduce the min. If such word exists, it returns its L, and since L is minimal among contained, it is also minimal among all forbidden words starting at i? Wait what if there is a shorter forbidden word starting at i but not fully contained in word[i:limit[i+1]]? If it is shorter, then i+L <= i+shorter_L < i+returned_L <= limit[i+1], so it would be contained. Contradiction. So returned L is the global minimum length among forbidden words starting at i. Thus limit[i] = min(limit[i+1], i+L-1). Since i+L <= limit[i+1], i+L-1 < limit[i+1], so limit[i] = i+L-1. The code sets right = i+L-1. Good.

One subtlety: What if there is a shorter forbidden word starting at i that is not a prefix of word[i:limit[i+1]] because the word at i doesn't match it? But "forbidden word starting at i" means it matches word[i:i+L]. So it is a prefix. Good.

Thus correct.

Now, let's consider if the final code should be exactly:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            node = trie
            for ch in f:
                if ch not in node:
                    node[ch] = {}
                node = node[ch]
            node["end"] = True

        def check(left: int, right: int) -> int:
            node = trie
            limit = min(right, left + max_len)
            for pos in range(left, limit):
                ch = word[pos]
                if ch not in node:
                    return 0
                node = node[ch]
                if "end" in node:
                    return pos - left + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        right = len(word)
        for left in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            length = check(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)
        return ans
```

This is good.

    right = min(right, left + length - 1)
```
Let's include min for safety. It doesn't change behavior. But if `length` is returned, `left+length-1` < right, so min = left+length-1. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0

        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            node = trie
            for ch in f:
                if ch not in node:
                    node[ch] = {}
                node = node[ch]
            node["end"] = True

        def forbidden_prefix_length(left: int, right: int) -> int:
            node = trie
            limit = min(right, left + max_len)

            for pos in range(left, limit):
                ch = word[pos]
                if ch not in node:
                    return 0
                node = node[ch]
                if "end" in node:
                    return pos - left + 1

            return 0

        ans = 0
        right = len(word)

        for left in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_length(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)

        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0

        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            node = trie
            for ch in f:
                if ch not in node:
                    node[ch] = {}
                node = node[ch]
            node["end"] = True

        def forbidden_prefix_length(left: int, right: int) -> int:
            node = trie
            limit = min(right, left + max_len)

            for pos in range(left, limit):
                ch = word[pos]
                if ch not in node:
                    return 0
                node = node[ch]
                if "end" in node:
                    return pos - left + 1

            return 0

        ans = 0
        right = len(word)

        for left in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_length(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)

        return ans
